1. Create the new subsystem

I'd add this beside your existing security system:

server/
├── security/
│   ├── ...
│
├── security_response/
│   ├── __init__.py
│   ├── models.py
│   ├── schemas.py
│   ├── classifier.py
│   ├── correlator.py
│   ├── risk_engine.py
│   ├── planner.py
│   ├── policy_guard.py
│   ├── orchestrator.py
│   ├── verifier.py
│   ├── reporter.py
│   ├── notification.py
│   │
│   ├── executors/
│   │   ├── __init__.py
│   │   ├── rate_limit.py
│   │   ├── session.py
│   │   ├── capability.py
│   │   └── quarantine.py
│   │
│   └── api/
│       ├── __init__.py
│       ├── websocket.py
│       └── incidents.py
│
└── core/
    └── events/
        └── ...

This keeps the response engine independent from Aariya's normal reasoning.

2. Define the security events

Your existing security/event system should feed this engine.

security_response/schemas.py

from datetime import datetime
from enum import Enum
from pydantic import BaseModel, Field


class AttackType(str, Enum):
    UNKNOWN = "unknown"
    BRUTE_FORCE = "brute_force"
    CREDENTIAL_ABUSE = "credential_abuse"
    PRIVILEGE_ESCALATION = "privilege_escalation"
    UNAUTHORIZED_ACCESS = "unauthorized_access"
    CAPABILITY_ABUSE = "capability_abuse"
    SESSION_ABUSE = "session_abuse"
    API_ABUSE = "api_abuse"
    ANOMALOUS_BEHAVIOR = "anomalous_behavior"


class Severity(str, Enum):
    LOW = "low"
    MEDIUM = "medium"
    HIGH = "high"
    CRITICAL = "critical"


class SecurityEvent(BaseModel):
    event_id: str
    timestamp: datetime

    source: str
    actor_id: str | None = None
    session_id: str | None = None
    resource: str | None = None
    action: str | None = None

    success: bool = False

    metadata: dict = Field(default_factory=dict)


class AttackAssessment(BaseModel):
    attack_type: AttackType
    severity: Severity
    confidence: float

    evidence: list[str]
    affected_resources: list[str]

    active: bool
    estimated_impact: str
3. Create the incident model

An incident represents the attack as a whole.

security_response/models.py

from datetime import datetime
from sqlalchemy import (
    String,
    DateTime,
    Float,
    Boolean,
    JSON,
    Text,
)
from sqlalchemy.orm import DeclarativeBase, Mapped, mapped_column


class Base(DeclarativeBase):
    pass


class SecurityIncident(Base):
    __tablename__ = "security_incidents"

    id: Mapped[str] = mapped_column(String(64), primary_key=True)

    created_at: Mapped[datetime] = mapped_column(
        DateTime(timezone=True)
    )

    updated_at: Mapped[datetime] = mapped_column(
        DateTime(timezone=True)
    )

    attack_type: Mapped[str] = mapped_column(String(64))
    severity: Mapped[str] = mapped_column(String(32))

    confidence: Mapped[float] = mapped_column(Float)

    status: Mapped[str] = mapped_column(
        String(32),
        default="detected"
    )

    active: Mapped[bool] = mapped_column(
        Boolean,
        default=True
    )

    evidence: Mapped[list] = mapped_column(JSON)

    affected_resources: Mapped[list] = mapped_column(JSON)

    final_report: Mapped[str | None] = mapped_column(
        Text,
        nullable=True
    )

You'll also want a separate table for every response action.

class CountermeasureExecution(Base):
    __tablename__ = "countermeasure_executions"

    id: Mapped[str] = mapped_column(String(64), primary_key=True)

    incident_id: Mapped[str] = mapped_column(String(64))

    action_type: Mapped[str] = mapped_column(String(64))

    target: Mapped[str] = mapped_column(String(256))

    status: Mapped[str] = mapped_column(String(32))

    reason: Mapped[str] = mapped_column(Text)

    started_at: Mapped[datetime] = mapped_column(
        DateTime(timezone=True)
    )

    completed_at: Mapped[datetime | None] = mapped_column(
        DateTime(timezone=True),
        nullable=True
    )

    result: Mapped[dict] = mapped_column(JSON)

This gives you a complete audit trail.

4. Attack classification

Don't immediately ask an LLM.

First use deterministic security signals.

classifier.py

from .schemas import (
    SecurityEvent,
    AttackAssessment,
    AttackType,
    Severity,
)


class AttackClassifier:

    def classify(
        self,
        events: list[SecurityEvent],
    ) -> AttackAssessment:

        failed_auth = sum(
            1
            for e in events
            if e.action == "authentication"
            and not e.success
        )

        privilege_attempts = sum(
            1
            for e in events
            if e.action == "privilege_escalation"
        )

        capability_violations = sum(
            1
            for e in events
            if e.action == "capability_execution"
            and not e.success
        )

        evidence = []

        if failed_auth >= 10:
            evidence.append(
                f"{failed_auth} failed authentication attempts"
            )

            return AttackAssessment(
                attack_type=AttackType.BRUTE_FORCE,
                severity=(
                    Severity.HIGH
                    if failed_auth >= 50
                    else Severity.MEDIUM
                ),
                confidence=min(
                    0.99,
                    0.70 + failed_auth / 100
                ),
                evidence=evidence,
                affected_resources=list({
                    e.resource
                    for e in events
                    if e.resource
                }),
                active=True,
                estimated_impact="Potential account compromise",
            )

        if privilege_attempts:
            evidence.append(
                f"{privilege_attempts} privilege escalation attempts"
            )

            return AttackAssessment(
                attack_type=AttackType.PRIVILEGE_ESCALATION,
                severity=Severity.HIGH,
                confidence=0.92,
                evidence=evidence,
                affected_resources=list({
                    e.resource
                    for e in events
                    if e.resource
                }),
                active=True,
                estimated_impact="Potential unauthorized privilege acquisition",
            )

        if capability_violations:
            evidence.append(
                f"{capability_violations} capability violations"
            )

            return AttackAssessment(
                attack_type=AttackType.CAPABILITY_ABUSE,
                severity=Severity.HIGH,
                confidence=0.90,
                evidence=evidence,
                affected_resources=list({
                    e.resource
                    for e in events
                    if e.resource
                }),
                active=True,
                estimated_impact="Potential agent capability compromise",
            )

        return AttackAssessment(
            attack_type=AttackType.ANOMALOUS_BEHAVIOR,
            severity=Severity.LOW,
            confidence=0.40,
            evidence=["No known attack signature matched"],
            affected_resources=[],
            active=True,
            estimated_impact="Unknown",
        )

Later, your ML/AI threat-analysis layer can make this much more sophisticated.

5. Risk engine

risk_engine.py

from .schemas import AttackAssessment, Severity


class RiskEngine:

    def calculate(self, assessment: AttackAssessment) -> int:

        severity_score = {
            Severity.LOW: 20,
            Severity.MEDIUM: 40,
            Severity.HIGH: 70,
            Severity.CRITICAL: 95,
        }[assessment.severity]

        confidence_score = assessment.confidence * 100

        return round(
            severity_score * 0.7 +
            confidence_score * 0.3
        )

Example:

Severity: HIGH
Confidence: 92%

Risk:
78
6. Countermeasure planner

This is the heart of what you're asking for.

planner.py

from dataclasses import dataclass
from .schemas import AttackAssessment, AttackType


@dataclass
class Countermeasure:
    action: str
    target: str
    reason: str
    priority: int
    reversible: bool


class CountermeasurePlanner:

    def plan(
        self,
        assessment: AttackAssessment,
    ) -> list[Countermeasure]:

        actions = []

        if assessment.attack_type == AttackType.BRUTE_FORCE:

            actions.append(
                Countermeasure(
                    action="rate_limit",
                    target="authentication",
                    reason="Reduce authentication abuse",
                    priority=1,
                    reversible=True,
                )
            )

            actions.append(
                Countermeasure(
                    action="require_reauthentication",
                    target="affected_sessions",
                    reason="Invalidate potentially compromised sessions",
                    priority=2,
                    reversible=True,
                )
            )

        elif assessment.attack_type == AttackType.PRIVILEGE_ESCALATION:

            actions.append(
                Countermeasure(
                    action="deny_capability",
                    target="attempted_privileged_capability",
                    reason="Prevent unauthorized privilege acquisition",
                    priority=1,
                    reversible=True,
                )
            )

            actions.append(
                Countermeasure(
                    action="quarantine_agent",
                    target="affected_agent",
                    reason="Contain potentially compromised agent",
                    priority=2,
                    reversible=True,
                )
            )

        elif assessment.attack_type == AttackType.CAPABILITY_ABUSE:

            actions.append(
                Countermeasure(
                    action="deny_capability",
                    target="violating_capability",
                    reason="Prevent repeated unauthorized execution",
                    priority=1,
                    reversible=True,
                )
            )

            actions.append(
                Countermeasure(
                    action="quarantine_agent",
                    target="affected_agent",
                    reason="Contain capability abuse",
                    priority=2,
                    reversible=True,
                )
            )

        return sorted(
            actions,
            key=lambda x: x.priority
        )
7. The Safety Governor

This is mandatory before autonomous execution.

policy_guard.py

from .planner import Countermeasure


class CountermeasurePolicy:

    ALLOWED_AUTONOMOUS_ACTIONS = {
        "rate_limit",
        "require_reauthentication",
        "deny_capability",
        "quarantine_agent",
    }

    def validate(
        self,
        action: Countermeasure,
    ) -> bool:

        if action.action not in self.ALLOWED_AUTONOMOUS_ACTIONS:
            return False

        if not action.reversible:
            return False

        return True

This means an AI cannot invent:

delete_database
disable_security
destroy_logs
modify_authorization_policy

and execute it.

Even if the LLM recommends something dangerous, the governor blocks it.

8. Countermeasure executors

For example:

executors/capability.py

class CapabilityExecutor:

    def __init__(self, permission_manager):
        self.permission_manager = permission_manager

    async def deny(
        self,
        capability: str,
        actor_id: str,
    ):

        await self.permission_manager.revoke_temporary(
            actor_id=actor_id,
            capability=capability,
            reason="security_incident",
        )

        return {
            "success": True,
            "action": "deny_capability",
            "capability": capability,
            "actor_id": actor_id,
        }

And:

executors/quarantine.py

class AgentQuarantineExecutor:

    def __init__(self, permission_manager):
        self.permission_manager = permission_manager

    async def quarantine(self, agent_id: str):

        await self.permission_manager.enter_quarantine(
            agent_id=agent_id,
            reason="active_security_incident",
        )

        return {
            "success": True,
            "agent_id": agent_id,
            "state": "quarantined",
        }

This connects directly to the Permission Manager you've already been designing.

9. Real-time event channel

Your mobile application needs to see what Aariya is doing while the attack is happening.

Redis Pub/Sub is a good fit for the transient live stream.

notification.py

import json
import redis.asyncio as redis


class SecurityEventPublisher:

    def __init__(self, redis_url: str):
        self.redis = redis.from_url(
            redis_url,
            decode_responses=True,
        )

    async def publish(
        self,
        incident_id: str,
        event_type: str,
        data: dict,
    ):

        payload = {
            "incident_id": incident_id,
            "event_type": event_type,
            "data": data,
        }

        await self.redis.publish(
            "aariya.security.live",
            json.dumps(payload),
        )
10. WebSocket to the mobile app

api/websocket.py

import json
from fastapi import (
    APIRouter,
    WebSocket,
    WebSocketDisconnect,
)
import redis.asyncio as redis


router = APIRouter()


@router.websocket("/ws/security")
async def security_websocket(
    websocket: WebSocket,
):

    await websocket.accept()

    client = redis.from_url(
        "redis://localhost:6379",
        decode_responses=True,
    )

    pubsub = client.pubsub()

    await pubsub.subscribe(
        "aariya.security.live"
    )

    try:

        async for message in pubsub.listen():

            if message["type"] != "message":
                continue

            await websocket.send_text(
                message["data"]
            )

    except WebSocketDisconnect:
        pass

    finally:
        await pubsub.unsubscribe(
            "aariya.security.live"
        )
        await client.close()

Your Flutter app connects to:

wss://your-aariya-server/ws/security

12. The orchestrator

Now combine everything.

orchestrator.py

import uuid
from datetime import datetime, timezone

from .classifier import AttackClassifier
from .risk_engine import RiskEngine
from .planner import CountermeasurePlanner
from .policy_guard import CountermeasurePolicy


class SecurityResponseOrchestrator:

    def __init__(
        self,
        classifier,
        risk_engine,
        planner,
        policy,
        publisher,
        executors,
    ):
        self.classifier = classifier
        self.risk_engine = risk_engine
        self.planner = planner
        self.policy = policy
        self.publisher = publisher
        self.executors = executors

    async def process(self, events):

        incident_id = (
            f"INC-{uuid.uuid4().hex[:16]}"
        )

        await self.publisher.publish(
            incident_id,
            "analysis_started",
            {},
        )

        assessment = self.classifier.classify(
            events
        )

        risk = self.risk_engine.calculate(
            assessment
        )

        await self.publisher.publish(
            incident_id,
            "attack_classified",
            {
                "attack_type":
                    assessment.attack_type.value,
                "severity":
                    assessment.severity.value,
                "confidence":
                    assessment.confidence,
                "risk": risk,
            },
        )

        plan = self.planner.plan(
            assessment
        )

        for countermeasure in plan:

            allowed = self.policy.validate(
                countermeasure
            )

            if not allowed:
                await self.publisher.publish(
                    incident_id,
                    "countermeasure_blocked",
                    {
                        "action":
                            countermeasure.action,
                        "reason":
                            "Security policy rejected action",
                    },
                )
                continue

            await self.publisher.publish(
                incident_id,
                "countermeasure_started",
                {
                    "action":
                        countermeasure.action,
                    "target":
                        countermeasure.target,
                    "reason":
                        countermeasure.reason,
                },
            )

            result = await self._execute(
                countermeasure
            )

            await self.publisher.publish(
                incident_id,
                "countermeasure_completed",
                {
                    "action":
                        countermeasure.action,
                    "result": result,
                },
            )

        await self.publisher.publish(
            incident_id,
            "incident_response_complete",
            {},
        )

        return incident_id

    async def _execute(self, countermeasure):

        executor = self.executors.get(
            countermeasure.action
        )

        if executor is None:
            return {
                "success": False,
                "error": "No executor available",
            }

        return await executor(
            countermeasure.target
        )
13. This must run continuously

Don't make this another normal Aariya conversation task.

Create a dedicated worker:

SecurityEvent
      │
      ▼
Event Bus
      │
      ▼
Security Response Queue
      │
      ▼
Response Worker
      │
      ├── classify
      ├── correlate
      ├── assess
      ├── plan
      ├── validate
      ├── execute
      └── verify

Your existing event bus should publish security events to it.

Conceptually:

async def security_event_handler(event):

    if not event.security_relevant:
        return

    await security_response_queue.publish(
        event
    )

The worker consumes these continuously.

14. Attack correlation

This is very important.

Don't treat:

failed login
failed login
failed login
failed login

as four unrelated incidents.

The correlator should combine events:

10:41:02 authentication failure
10:41:03 authentication failure
10:41:04 authentication failure
10:41:05 suspicious API request
10:41:06 session anomaly

into:

INC-001

Likely attack chain:

Credential Abuse
       ↓
Authentication Attack
       ↓
Session Acquisition
       ↓
API Abuse

This is much closer to a real security operations system.

15. Verification

Aariya must never assume the countermeasure worked.

After executing:

Countermeasure
      ↓
Wait/observe
      ↓
Collect new security events
      ↓
Compare attack activity
      ↓
Did attack stop?

Example:

class ResponseVerifier:

    def verify(
        self,
        before_count: int,
        after_count: int,
    ) -> bool:

        return after_count < before_count

In your real implementation, this should use actual event windows and attack-specific success criteria.

For example:

Before:
150 authentication failures / minute

Countermeasure:
Rate limit activated

After:
3 authentication failures / minute

Result:
SUCCESS

If activity continues:

Countermeasure 1
       ↓
FAILED
       ↓
Recalculate threat
       ↓
Countermeasure 2
       ↓
Verify

That gives you adaptive response.

16. Away mode

This is where your requirement becomes particularly useful.

Aariya should maintain:

user_presence
    ONLINE
    AWAY
    OFFLINE

When you're online:

Attack
 ↓
Aariya responds
 ↓
Mobile receives live stream

When you're away:

Attack
 ↓
Aariya responds autonomously
 ↓
Incident continues
 ↓
Aariya records everything
 ↓
Attack contained
 ↓
Generate report
 ↓
Send notification

18. Mobile notification

For an active attack, WebSocket gives you the live interface.

For an attack that happens while you're away, you also need a persistent push notification channel.

The architecture becomes:

                    Security Engine
                          │
                ┌─────────┴─────────┐
                │                   │
             Redis              Notification
             Pub/Sub             Service
                │                   │
                ▼                   ▼
          Live WebSocket       Push notification
                │                   │
                └────────┬──────────┘
                         ▼
                    Flutter App

The push notification should be concise:

🚨 Aariya Security Alert

A high-severity privilege escalation
attempt was detected.

Aariya automatically contained the threat.

Tap to view the incident report.

The detailed report stays on your Aariya server and is retrieved securely after authentication.

19. Don't use the LLM for the first response decision

You specifically want autonomous behavior.

I'd implement the decision hierarchy like this:

                    EVENT
                      │
                      ▼
             Deterministic Detection
                      │
                      ▼
              Attack Classification
                      │
                      ▼
                 Risk Engine
                      │
                      ▼
              Rule-Based Planner
                      │
                      ▼
               Policy Governor
                      │
                      ▼
             Autonomous Response
                      │
                      ▼
                Verification
                      │
                      ▼
             ┌────────┴────────┐
             │                 │
           SUCCESS           FAILURE
             │                 │
             ▼                 ▼
          Close          Re-analyze
                              │
                              ▼
                       Second response

Then use the LLM above this layer for deeper analysis:

Security events
      ↓
Deterministic engine
      ↓
Attack identified
      ↓
LLM threat analyst
      ↓
Additional reasoning
      ↓
Structured recommendation
      ↓
Policy Governor
      ↓
Allowed action only

That prevents an LLM hallucination from becoming a security action.

20. Recommended complete architecture for Aariya

With the systems you've already been designing, I'd make the final architecture:

                         ┌────────────────────┐
                         │      AARIYA        │
                         │   Cognitive Core   │
                         └─────────┬──────────┘
                                   │
                                   │
                         ┌─────────▼─────────┐
                         │   Security Event  │
                         │       Bus         │
                         └─────────┬─────────┘
                                   │
                 ┌─────────────────┴─────────────────┐
                 │                                   │
                 ▼                                   ▼
       EXISTING SECURITY SYSTEM          SECURITY RESPONSE ENGINE
                 │                                   │
        Authorization                     ┌───────────▼───────────┐
        Permission Manager                │ Event Correlator      │
        RBAC / ABAC                       ├───────────────────────┤
        Credential Security               │ Attack Classifier     │
        Policy Engine                     ├───────────────────────┤
        Threat Detection                  │ Risk Engine           │
        Audit                             ├───────────────────────┤
                 │                        │ Response Planner      │
                 │                        ├───────────────────────┤
                 │                        │ Policy Governor       │
                 │                        ├───────────────────────┤
                 │                        │ Countermeasure        │
                 │                        │ Executors             │
                 │                        ├───────────────────────┤
                 │                        │ Response Verifier     │
                 │                        ├───────────────────────┤
                 │                        │ Incident Reporter     │
                 │                        └───────────┬───────────┘
                 │                                    │
                 └────────────────┬───────────────────┘
                                  │
                           PostgreSQL + Redis
                                  │
                    ┌─────────────┴─────────────┐
                    │                           │
                    ▼                           ▼
             Incident History            Live Event Stream
                                                │
                                                ▼
                                          Flutter Mobile
                                                │
                                  ┌─────────────┴────────────┐
                                  │                          │
                              LIVE MODE                  AWAY MODE
                                  │                          │
                         Live countermeasures         Push notification
                         Attack status                Incident report
                         Risk changes                Full timeline
                         Verification                Actions taken


Milestone 1 — Security Response Foundation

Now we build the actual foundation of the parallel autonomous response system.

The objective of this milestone is:

Existing Aariya Security System
          │
          ▼
      Security Event
          │
          ▼
┌──────────────────────────┐
│ Security Response Engine │
│                          │
│ Event Intake             │
│ Incident Creation        │
│ Persistence              │
│ Live Event Publishing    │
└────────────┬─────────────┘
             │
             ├── PostgreSQL → permanent incident history
             │
             └── Redis → live security stream

We are not yet allowing autonomous countermeasures. That comes after the foundation is reliable.

1. Directory

Add:

server/

2. Configuration

security_response/config.py

from pydantic_settings import BaseSettings, SettingsConfigDict


class SecurityResponseSettings(BaseSettings):
    database_url: str
    redis_url: str

    security_event_channel: str = "aariya.security.events"
    security_live_channel: str = "aariya.security.live"

    model_config = SettingsConfigDict(
        env_prefix="AARIYA_SECURITY_",
        env_file=".env",
        extra="ignore",
    )


settings = SecurityResponseSettings()

Your .env should contain real infrastructure values:

AARIYA_SECURITY_DATABASE_URL=postgresql+asyncpg://USER:PASSWORD@HOST:5432/aariya
AARIYA_SECURITY_REDIS_URL=redis://HOST:6379/0

Don't put credentials directly into Python.

3. Security event schema

security_response/schemas.py

from datetime import datetime
from enum import Enum

from pydantic import BaseModel, Field


class SecurityEventType(str, Enum):
    AUTHENTICATION_FAILURE = "authentication_failure"
    AUTHENTICATION_SUCCESS = "authentication_success"

    AUTHORIZATION_DENIED = "authorization_denied"
    PRIVILEGE_ESCALATION_ATTEMPT = "privilege_escalation_attempt"

    CAPABILITY_DENIED = "capability_denied"
    CAPABILITY_EXECUTION = "capability_execution"

    SESSION_ANOMALY = "session_anomaly"
    API_ANOMALY = "api_anomaly"

    CREDENTIAL_ANOMALY = "credential_anomaly"

    POLICY_VIOLATION = "policy_violation"
    SECURITY_TAMPERING = "security_tampering"

    SYSTEM_ANOMALY = "system_anomaly"


class SecurityEvent(BaseModel):
    event_id: str
    event_type: SecurityEventType

    timestamp: datetime

    source_service: str

    actor_id: str | None = None
    session_id: str | None = None
    resource: str | None = None
    capability: str | None = None

    success: bool | None = None

    severity_hint: str | None = None

    metadata: dict = Field(default_factory=dict)

Notice that we're keeping this generic.

The existing security system can generate events without needing to know how the response engine works.

4. Incident states

Add:

from enum import Enum


class IncidentStatus(str, Enum):
    DETECTED = "detected"
    ANALYZING = "analyzing"
    ACTIVE = "active"
    CONTAINING = "containing"
    CONTAINED = "contained"
    RESOLVED = "resolved"
    ESCALATED = "escalated"
    FAILED = "failed"

Don't use a simple boolean such as:

attack = True

because eventually the incident has a lifecycle.

5. Database models

security_response/models.py

from datetime import datetime

from sqlalchemy import (
    Boolean,
    DateTime,
    Float,
    Integer,
    JSON,
    String,
    Text,
)
from sqlalchemy.orm import DeclarativeBase
from sqlalchemy.orm import Mapped
from sqlalchemy.orm import mapped_column


class Base(DeclarativeBase):
    pass


class SecurityIncident(Base):
    __tablename__ = "security_incidents"

    id: Mapped[str] = mapped_column(
        String(64),
        primary_key=True,
    )

    created_at: Mapped[datetime] = mapped_column(
        DateTime(timezone=True),
        nullable=False,
    )

    updated_at: Mapped[datetime] = mapped_column(
        DateTime(timezone=True),
        nullable=False,
    )

    status: Mapped[str] = mapped_column(
        String(32),
        nullable=False,
    )

    attack_type: Mapped[str | None] = mapped_column(
        String(64),
        nullable=True,
    )

    severity: Mapped[str | None] = mapped_column(
        String(32),
        nullable=True,
    )

    confidence: Mapped[float | None] = mapped_column(
        Float,
        nullable=True,
    )

    active: Mapped[bool] = mapped_column(
        Boolean,
        nullable=False,
        default=True,
    )

    event_count: Mapped[int] = mapped_column(
        Integer,
        nullable=False,
        default=0,
    )

    affected_resources: Mapped[list] = mapped_column(
        JSON,
        nullable=False,
        default=list,
    )

    metadata: Mapped[dict] = mapped_column(
        JSON,
        nullable=False,
        default=dict,
    )


class SecurityIncidentEvent(Base):
    __tablename__ = "security_incident_events"

    id: Mapped[str] = mapped_column(
        String(64),
        primary_key=True,
    )

    incident_id: Mapped[str] = mapped_column(
        String(64),
        nullable=False,
        index=True,
    )

    event_id: Mapped[str] = mapped_column(
        String(64),
        nullable=False,
        unique=True,
    )

    event_type: Mapped[str] = mapped_column(
        String(64),
        nullable=False,
    )

    timestamp: Mapped[datetime] = mapped_column(
        DateTime(timezone=True),
        nullable=False,
    )

    source_service: Mapped[str] = mapped_column(
        String(128),
        nullable=False,
    )

    payload: Mapped[dict] = mapped_column(
        JSON,
        nullable=False,
    )


class CountermeasureExecution(Base):
    __tablename__ = "countermeasure_executions"

    id: Mapped[str] = mapped_column(
        String(64),
        primary_key=True,
    )

    incident_id: Mapped[str] = mapped_column(
        String(64),
        nullable=False,
        index=True,
    )

    action_type: Mapped[str] = mapped_column(
        String(64),
        nullable=False,
    )

    target: Mapped[str | None] = mapped_column(
        String(256),
        nullable=True,
    )

    status: Mapped[str] = mapped_column(
        String(32),
        nullable=False,
    )

    reason: Mapped[str] = mapped_column(
        Text,
        nullable=False,
    )

    result: Mapped[dict] = mapped_column(
        JSON,
        nullable=False,
        default=dict,
    )

    started_at: Mapped[datetime] = mapped_column(
        DateTime(timezone=True),
        nullable=False,
    )

    completed_at: Mapped[datetime | None] = mapped_column(
        DateTime(timezone=True),
        nullable=True,
    )

CountermeasureExecution is being created now even though we won't execute anything yet.

That prevents us from redesigning the database later.

6. Repository

security_response/repository.py

from datetime import datetime, timezone
from uuid import uuid4

from sqlalchemy import select
from sqlalchemy.ext.asyncio import AsyncSession

from .models import (
    SecurityIncident,
    SecurityIncidentEvent,
)
from .schemas import SecurityEvent


class SecurityResponseRepository:

    def __init__(self, session: AsyncSession):
        self.session = session

    async def create_incident(
        self,
        event: SecurityEvent,
    ) -> SecurityIncident:

        now = datetime.now(timezone.utc)

        incident = SecurityIncident(
            id=f"INC-{uuid4().hex[:16]}",
            created_at=now,
            updated_at=now,
            status="detected",
            active=True,
            event_count=0,
            affected_resources=[],
            metadata={},
        )

        self.session.add(incident)

        await self.session.flush()

        await self.add_event(
            incident.id,
            event,
        )

        return incident

    async def add_event(
        self,
        incident_id: str,
        event: SecurityEvent,
    ):

        incident_event = SecurityIncidentEvent(
            id=f"EVT-{uuid4().hex[:16]}",
            incident_id=incident_id,
            event_id=event.event_id,
            event_type=event.event_type.value,
            timestamp=event.timestamp,
            source_service=event.source_service,
            payload=event.model_dump(mode="json"),
        )

        self.session.add(incident_event)

        result = await self.session.execute(
            select(SecurityIncident).where(
                SecurityIncident.id == incident_id
            )
        )

        incident = result.scalar_one()

        incident.event_count += 1
        incident.updated_at = datetime.now(timezone.utc)

        if event.resource:
            resources = set(
                incident.affected_resources or []
            )

            resources.add(event.resource)

            incident.affected_resources = list(
                resources
            )

        await self.session.flush()

        return incident
7. Live event publisher

security_response/publisher.py

import json

import redis.asyncio as redis

from .config import settings


class SecurityResponsePublisher:

    def __init__(self):
        self.redis = redis.from_url(
            settings.redis_url,
            decode_responses=True,
        )

    async def publish(
        self,
        event_type: str,
        incident_id: str,
        data: dict,
    ):

        payload = {
            "event_type": event_type,
            "incident_id": incident_id,
            "data": data,
        }

        await self.redis.publish(
            settings.security_live_channel,
            json.dumps(payload),
        )

    async def close(self):
        await self.redis.aclose()

This becomes the bridge between the backend and the mobile security interface.

8. Response service

security_response/service.py

from sqlalchemy.ext.asyncio import AsyncSession

from .publisher import SecurityResponsePublisher
from .repository import SecurityResponseRepository
from .schemas import SecurityEvent


class SecurityResponseService:

    def __init__(
        self,
        session: AsyncSession,
        publisher: SecurityResponsePublisher,
    ):
        self.repository = SecurityResponseRepository(
            session
        )

        self.publisher = publisher

    async def ingest(
        self,
        event: SecurityEvent,
    ):

        incident = await self.repository.create_incident(
            event
        )

        await self.publisher.publish(
            event_type="incident_detected",
            incident_id=incident.id,
            data={
                "source_service":
                    event.source_service,
                "event_type":
                    event.event_type.value,
                "resource":
                    event.resource,
                "severity_hint":
                    event.severity_hint,
            },
        )

        return incident.id

At this point:

Security Event
      ↓
Incident created
      ↓
Saved in PostgreSQL
      ↓
Live event sent to Redis
9. Worker

security_response/worker.py

The worker consumes events independently from Aariya's main cognition system.

import json

import redis.asyncio as redis

from sqlalchemy.ext.asyncio import (
    AsyncSession,
    async_sessionmaker,
    create_async_engine,
)

from .config import settings
from .publisher import SecurityResponsePublisher
from .schemas import SecurityEvent
from .service import SecurityResponseService


engine = create_async_engine(
    settings.database_url,
    pool_pre_ping=True,
)

SessionFactory = async_sessionmaker(
    engine,
    expire_on_commit=False,
)


async def run_worker():

    redis_client = redis.from_url(
        settings.redis_url,
        decode_responses=True,
    )

    pubsub = redis_client.pubsub()

    await pubsub.subscribe(
        settings.security_event_channel
    )

    publisher = SecurityResponsePublisher()

    try:

        async for message in pubsub.listen():

            if message["type"] != "message":
                continue

            payload = json.loads(
                message["data"]
            )

            event = SecurityEvent.model_validate(
                payload
            )

            async with SessionFactory() as session:

                service = SecurityResponseService(
                    session=session,
                    publisher=publisher,
                )

                try:

                    await service.ingest(event)

                    await session.commit()

                except Exception:

                    await session.rollback()

                    raise

    finally:

        await pubsub.unsubscribe(
            settings.security_event_channel
        )

        await redis_client.aclose()

        await publisher.close()
10. FastAPI WebSocket

security_response/api/websocket.py

import redis.asyncio as redis

from fastapi import (
    APIRouter,
    WebSocket,
    WebSocketDisconnect,
)

from ..config import settings


router = APIRouter()


@router.websocket("/ws/security")
async def security_stream(
    websocket: WebSocket,
):

    await websocket.accept()

    client = redis.from_url(
        settings.redis_url,
        decode_responses=True,
    )

    pubsub = client.pubsub()

    await pubsub.subscribe(
        settings.security_live_channel
    )

    try:

        async for message in pubsub.listen():

            if message["type"] != "message":
                continue

            await websocket.send_text(
                message["data"]
            )

    except WebSocketDisconnect:
        pass

    finally:

        await pubsub.unsubscribe(
            settings.security_live_channel
        )

        await client.aclose()

Important: this endpoint must eventually use your existing Aariya WebSocket authentication/JWT layer. Do not expose the security stream anonymously.

11. Incident API

security_response/api/incidents.py

from fastapi import APIRouter, Depends, HTTPException
from sqlalchemy import select
from sqlalchemy.ext.asyncio import AsyncSession

from ..models import SecurityIncident
from ..models import SecurityIncidentEvent


router = APIRouter(
    prefix="/security/incidents",
    tags=["security-incidents"],
)


@router.get("/{incident_id}")
async def get_incident(
    incident_id: str,
    session: AsyncSession,
):

    result = await session.execute(
        select(SecurityIncident).where(
            SecurityIncident.id == incident_id
        )
    )

    incident = result.scalar_one_or_none()

    if incident is None:
        raise HTTPException(
            status_code=404,
            detail="Incident not found",
        )

    events_result = await session.execute(
        select(SecurityIncidentEvent)
        .where(
            SecurityIncidentEvent.incident_id
            == incident_id
        )
        .order_by(
            SecurityIncidentEvent.timestamp
        )
    )

    events = events_result.scalars().all()

    return {
        "incident": {
            "id": incident.id,
            "status": incident.status,
            "created_at": incident.created_at,
            "updated_at": incident.updated_at,
            "attack_type": incident.attack_type,
            "severity": incident.severity,
            "confidence": incident.confidence,
            "active": incident.active,
            "event_count": incident.event_count,
            "affected_resources":
                incident.affected_resources,
        },
        "events": [
            {
                "id": event.event_id,
                "type": event.event_type,
                "timestamp": event.timestamp,
                "source": event.source_service,
                "payload": event.payload,
            }
            for event in events
        ],
    }

You should add your existing authentication dependency here before exposing this endpoint.

12. Connect it to Aariya's existing Event Bus

This is the important integration point.

Your existing security system should publish something like:

await event_bus.publish(
    SecurityEvent(
        event_id="...",
        event_type="authorization_denied",
        timestamp=datetime.now(timezone.utc),
        source_service="permission_manager",
        actor_id=actor_id,
        resource=resource,
        capability=capability,
        success=False,
        metadata={
            "reason": "permission_denied",
        },
    )
)

The Event Bus then forwards security-relevant events to:

aariya.security.events

The response worker is listening there.

13. What happens now

Once Milestone 1 is running:

Attacker / suspicious behavior
             │
             ▼
Existing Aariya Security
             │
             ▼
Security Event Bus
             │
             ▼
Redis
"aariya.security.events"
             │
             ▼
Security Response Worker
             │
             ├───────────────┐
             ▼               ▼
        PostgreSQL         Redis
        Incident DB        Live channel
             │               │
             │               ▼
             │          Mobile App
             │
             ▼
        Incident History

But nothing is automatically blocked yet.

That's intentional.

15. One correction before Milestone 2

There is one architectural issue we should not carry forward from the earlier prototype: creating a new incident for every security event.

In the real system, the next milestone needs to introduce:

Event
 ↓
Correlation Window
 ↓
Existing active incident?
 ├── YES → attach event
 └── NO  → create incident

Otherwise 500 failed login attempts could create 500 incidents.

So Milestone 2 should be the Event Correlation + Attack Classification layer, where we turn raw events into an actual attack narrative:

500 authentication failures
        +
3 suspicious sessions
        +
unusual API calls
        ↓
┌────────────────────────────┐
│ ONE SECURITY INCIDENT      │
│                            │
│ Attack: Credential Abuse   │
│ Confidence: 96%            │
│ Severity: HIGH             │
│ Status: ACTIVE             │
└────────────────────────────┘


Milestone 2 — Event Correlation + Attack Intelligence

Now we turn raw security events into one continuously analyzed incident.

Aariya should be able to distinguish:

10 failed logins
10 separate events
          ↓
one correlated attack
          ↓
Credential Abuse / Brute Force

And it should also recognize a sequence such as:

authentication failures
        ↓
successful login
        ↓
session anomaly
        ↓
permission denial
        ↓
privilege escalation attempt

as potentially one attack chain, rather than unrelated events.

For the classification vocabulary, use a structured threat model rather than inventing arbitrary names. MITRE ATT&CK currently includes Brute Force (T1110 and sub-techniques) under Credential Access and separate Privilege Escalation techniques, which makes it useful as a classification reference.

1. Add the correlation layer

Update the structure:

server/
└── security_response/
    ├── correlation/
    │   ├── __init__.py
    │   ├── key.py
    │   ├── window.py
    │   └── correlator.py
    │
    ├── intelligence/
    │   ├── __init__.py
    │   ├── classifier.py
    │   ├── scoring.py
    │   └── attack_chain.py
    │
    ├── schemas.py
    ├── models.py
    ├── repository.py
    ├── publisher.py
    ├── service.py
    └── worker.py
2. Correlation key

Events need to be grouped according to meaningful relationships.

Create correlation/key.py:

from security_response.schemas import SecurityEvent


def build_correlation_key(event: SecurityEvent) -> str:
    """
    Builds a stable key used to associate related
    security events with the same potential incident.
    """

    actor = event.actor_id or "unknown-actor"
    resource = event.resource or "unknown-resource"
    session = event.session_id or "no-session"

    return (
        f"{event.source_service}:"
        f"{actor}:"
        f"{resource}:"
        f"{session}"
    )

But don't rely only on this key.

An attacker could use multiple sessions or sources, so correlation also needs temporal and behavioral relationships.

3. Correlation window

Create correlation/window.py:

from datetime import datetime, timedelta

from security_response.schemas import SecurityEvent


class CorrelationWindow:

    def __init__(
        self,
        duration_seconds: int = 120,
    ):
        self.duration = timedelta(
            seconds=duration_seconds
        )

    def contains(
        self,
        first_event: SecurityEvent,
        new_event: SecurityEvent,
    ) -> bool:

        difference = (
            new_event.timestamp -
            first_event.timestamp
        )

        return (
            timedelta(0)
            <= difference
            <= self.duration
        )

This means events occurring close together can belong to the same incident.

Later, different attack types will have different windows.

For example:

Authentication attack:
1–5 minutes

Privilege escalation:
30 seconds–2 minutes

Slow reconnaissance:
10–30 minutes
4. Attack classification model

Expand schemas.py.

from enum import Enum


class AttackType(str, Enum):

    UNKNOWN = "unknown"

    BRUTE_FORCE = "brute_force"
    PASSWORD_SPRAYING = "password_spraying"
    CREDENTIAL_STUFFING = "credential_stuffing"

    UNAUTHORIZED_ACCESS = "unauthorized_access"

    PRIVILEGE_ESCALATION = "privilege_escalation"

    CAPABILITY_ABUSE = "capability_abuse"

    SESSION_HIJACKING = "session_hijacking"

    API_ABUSE = "api_abuse"

    CREDENTIAL_ABUSE = "credential_abuse"

    POLICY_TAMPERING = "policy_tampering"

    ANOMALOUS_BEHAVIOR = "anomalous_behavior"

    MULTI_STAGE_ATTACK = "multi_stage_attack"

MITRE specifically distinguishes password guessing, password spraying and credential stuffing as sub-techniques of Brute Force, so keeping them separate gives Aariya much better response context later.

5. Attack assessment

Add:

from pydantic import BaseModel, Field


class AttackEvidence(BaseModel):
    event_ids: list[str] = Field(
        default_factory=list
    )

    observations: list[str] = Field(
        default_factory=list
    )


class AttackAssessment(BaseModel):

    attack_type: AttackType

    confidence: float

    risk_score: float

    severity: str

    active: bool

    evidence: AttackEvidence

    affected_actors: list[str] = Field(
        default_factory=list
    )

    affected_resources: list[str] = Field(
        default_factory=list
    )

    attack_stage: str | None = None

    mitre_techniques: list[str] = Field(
        default_factory=list
    )

Now an incident has a proper analytical state.

6. Correlator

Create correlation/correlator.py.

from dataclasses import dataclass, field

from security_response.schemas import SecurityEvent

from .window import CorrelationWindow


@dataclass
class EventCluster:

    cluster_id: str

    events: list[SecurityEvent] = field(
        default_factory=list
    )

    def add(
        self,
        event: SecurityEvent,
    ) -> None:

        self.events.append(event)

    @property
    def first_event(self) -> SecurityEvent:
        return self.events[0]


class EventCorrelator:

    def __init__(
        self,
        window_seconds: int = 120,
    ):
        self.window = CorrelationWindow(
            window_seconds
        )

        self.clusters: dict[
            str,
            EventCluster
        ] = {}

    def correlate(
        self,
        event: SecurityEvent,
    ) -> EventCluster:

        for cluster in self.clusters.values():

            if not self.window.contains(
                cluster.first_event,
                event,
            ):
                continue

            if self._related(
                cluster.events[-1],
                event,
            ):
                cluster.add(event)

                return cluster

        cluster_id = (
            f"CLUSTER-{event.event_id}"
        )

        cluster = EventCluster(
            cluster_id=cluster_id,
            events=[event],
        )

        self.clusters[
            cluster_id
        ] = cluster

        return cluster

    def _related(
        self,
        previous: SecurityEvent,
        current: SecurityEvent,
    ) -> bool:

        if (
            previous.actor_id
            and current.actor_id
            and previous.actor_id
            == current.actor_id
        ):
            return True

        if (
            previous.session_id
            and current.session_id
            and previous.session_id
            == current.session_id
        ):
            return True

        if (
            previous.resource
            and current.resource
            and previous.resource
            == current.resource
        ):
            return True

        return (
            previous.source_service
            == current.source_service
        )

This is deliberately conservative for now.

7. Don't correlate forever

The in-memory cluster must expire.

Otherwise Aariya's memory grows forever.

Add:

from datetime import datetime, timezone


class EventCorrelator:

    # ...

    def cleanup(self) -> None:

        now = datetime.now(timezone.utc)

        expired = []

        for cluster_id, cluster in self.clusters.items():

            last_event = cluster.events[-1]

            if (
                now - last_event.timestamp
                > self.window.duration
            ):
                expired.append(cluster_id)

        for cluster_id in expired:
            del self.clusters[cluster_id]

Later Redis should hold distributed correlation state so multiple response workers can share it.

8. First attack detector

Create intelligence/classifier.py.

from collections import Counter

from security_response.schemas import (
    AttackAssessment,
    AttackEvidence,
    AttackType,
    SecurityEvent,
)


class AttackClassifier:

    def classify(
        self,
        events: list[SecurityEvent],
    ) -> AttackAssessment:

        types = Counter(
            event.event_type
            for event in events
        )

        failed_auth = types.get(
            "authentication_failure",
            0,
        )

        privilege_attempts = types.get(
            "privilege_escalation_attempt",
            0,
        )

        capability_denials = types.get(
            "capability_denied",
            0,
        )

        policy_violations = types.get(
            "policy_violation",
            0,
        )

        # -----------------------------
        # Brute force
        # -----------------------------

        if failed_auth >= 10:

            confidence = min(
                0.99,
                0.70 + (
                    failed_auth / 100
                ),
            )

            severity = (
                "critical"
                if failed_auth >= 100
                else "high"
                if failed_auth >= 50
                else "medium"
            )

            return AttackAssessment(
                attack_type=AttackType.BRUTE_FORCE,
                confidence=confidence,
                risk_score=0,
                severity=severity,
                active=True,
                evidence=AttackEvidence(
                    event_ids=[
                        e.event_id
                        for e in events
                    ],
                    observations=[
                        f"{failed_auth} authentication failures"
                    ],
                ),
                affected_actors=[
                    e.actor_id
                    for e in events
                    if e.actor_id
                ],
                affected_resources=[
                    e.resource
                    for e in events
                    if e.resource
                ],
                attack_stage="credential_access",
                mitre_techniques=[
                    "T1110"
                ],
            )

        # -----------------------------
        # Privilege escalation
        # -----------------------------

        if privilege_attempts > 0:

            return AttackAssessment(
                attack_type=AttackType.PRIVILEGE_ESCALATION,
                confidence=0.92,
                risk_score=0,
                severity="high",
                active=True,
                evidence=AttackEvidence(
                    event_ids=[
                        e.event_id
                        for e in events
                    ],
                    observations=[
                        f"{privilege_attempts} privilege escalation attempts"
                    ],
                ),
                affected_actors=[
                    e.actor_id
                    for e in events
                    if e.actor_id
                ],
                affected_resources=[
                    e.resource
                    for e in events
                    if e.resource
                ],
                attack_stage="privilege_escalation",
                mitre_techniques=[],
            )

        # -----------------------------
        # Capability abuse
        # -----------------------------

        if capability_denials >= 3:

            return AttackAssessment(
                attack_type=AttackType.CAPABILITY_ABUSE,
                confidence=0.88,
                risk_score=0,
                severity="high",
                active=True,
                evidence=AttackEvidence(
                    event_ids=[
                        e.event_id
                        for e in events
                    ],
                    observations=[
                        f"{capability_denials} denied capability attempts"
                    ],
                ),
                affected_actors=[
                    e.actor_id
                    for e in events
                    if e.actor_id
                ],
                affected_resources=[
                    e.resource
                    for e in events
                    if e.resource
                ],
                attack_stage="execution",
                mitre_techniques=[],
            )

        # -----------------------------
        # Policy tampering
        # -----------------------------

        if policy_violations > 0:

            return AttackAssessment(
                attack_type=AttackType.POLICY_TAMPERING,
                confidence=0.95,
                risk_score=0,
                severity="critical",
                active=True,
                evidence=AttackEvidence(
                    event_ids=[
                        e.event_id
                        for e in events
                    ],
                    observations=[
                        "Security policy violation detected"
                    ],
                ),
                affected_actors=[
                    e.actor_id
                    for e in events
                    if e.actor_id
                ],
                affected_resources=[
                    e.resource
                    for e in events
                    if e.resource
                ],
                attack_stage="defense_impairment",
                mitre_techniques=[],
            )

        return AttackAssessment(
            attack_type=AttackType.ANOMALOUS_BEHAVIOR,
            confidence=0.35,
            risk_score=0,
            severity="low",
            active=True,
            evidence=AttackEvidence(
                event_ids=[
                    e.event_id
                    for e in events
                ],
                observations=[
                    "No high-confidence attack signature matched"
                ],
            ),
            affected_actors=[
                e.actor_id
                for e in events
                if e.actor_id
            ],
            affected_resources=[
                e.resource
                for e in events
                if e.resource
            ],
        )
9. Risk scoring

intelligence/scoring.py

class RiskScorer:

    SEVERITY_BASE = {
        "low": 20,
        "medium": 45,
        "high": 70,
        "critical": 95,
    }

    def calculate(
        self,
        severity: str,
        confidence: float,
        event_count: int,
        affected_resource_count: int,
    ) -> float:

        base = self.SEVERITY_BASE.get(
            severity,
            20,
        )

        confidence_component = (
            confidence * 100
        )

        frequency_component = min(
            event_count * 0.5,
            10,
        )

        spread_component = min(
            affected_resource_count * 2,
            10,
        )

        score = (
            base * 0.55
            + confidence_component * 0.30
            + frequency_component
            + spread_component
        )

        return round(
            min(score, 100),
            2,
        )

For example:

Severity       HIGH
Confidence     92%
Events         42
Resources      2

Risk           84.4

The number is a prioritization signal, not a claim that the system knows the exact probability of compromise.

10. Attack-chain detection

This is where the system starts becoming significantly more intelligent.

Create intelligence/attack_chain.py:

from security_response.schemas import (
    AttackType,
    SecurityEvent,
)


class AttackChainDetector:

    def detect(
        self,
        events: list[SecurityEvent],
    ) -> AttackType | None:

        event_types = [
            event.event_type
            for event in events
        ]

        auth_failure = (
            "authentication_failure"
            in event_types
        )

        auth_success = (
            "authentication_success"
            in event_types
        )

        session_anomaly = (
            "session_anomaly"
            in event_types
        )

        privilege_attempt = (
            "privilege_escalation_attempt"
            in event_types
        )

        policy_violation = (
            "policy_violation"
            in event_types
        )

        if (
            auth_failure
            and auth_success
            and session_anomaly
        ):
            return AttackType.CREDENTIAL_ABUSE

        if (
            privilege_attempt
            and session_anomaly
        ):
            return AttackType.MULTI_STAGE_ATTACK

        if (
            policy_violation
            and privilege_attempt
        ):
            return AttackType.MULTI_STAGE_ATTACK

        return None

Now Aariya can differentiate:

Single Event:
Privilege escalation

from:

Attack chain:

Authentication anomaly
        ↓
Session anomaly
        ↓
Privilege escalation
        ↓
Policy violation

        ↓

MULTI-STAGE ATTACK
11. Combine classifier + chain detector

Update the intelligence service.

intelligence/__init__.py

from .classifier import AttackClassifier
from .attack_chain import AttackChainDetector
from .scoring import RiskScorer


class AttackIntelligence:

    def __init__(self):

        self.classifier = AttackClassifier()

        self.chain_detector = (
            AttackChainDetector()
        )

        self.scorer = RiskScorer()

    def analyze(self, events):

        assessment = self.classifier.classify(
            events
        )

        chain_type = (
            self.chain_detector.detect(
                events
            )
        )

        if chain_type is not None:

            assessment.attack_type = chain_type
            assessment.severity = "high"

            assessment.evidence.observations.append(
                "Multiple related security events form an attack chain."
            )

        assessment.risk_score = (
            self.scorer.calculate(
                severity=assessment.severity,
                confidence=assessment.confidence,
                event_count=len(events),
                affected_resource_count=len(
                    assessment.affected_resources
                ),
            )
        )

        return assessment
12. Update the incident

The incident shouldn't remain:

status = detected

After analysis:

detected
   ↓
analyzing
   ↓
active

Add this to repository.py:

async def update_assessment(
    self,
    incident_id: str,
    assessment,
):

    result = await self.session.execute(
        select(SecurityIncident).where(
            SecurityIncident.id == incident_id
        )
    )

    incident = result.scalar_one()

    incident.attack_type = (
        assessment.attack_type.value
    )

    incident.severity = (
        assessment.severity
    )

    incident.confidence = (
        assessment.confidence
    )

    incident.status = "active"

    incident.metadata = {
        **(incident.metadata or {}),
        "risk_score": assessment.risk_score,
        "attack_stage":
            assessment.attack_stage,
        "mitre_techniques":
            assessment.mitre_techniques,
        "evidence":
            assessment.evidence.model_dump(),
    }

    incident.updated_at = datetime.now(
        timezone.utc
    )

    await self.session.flush()

    return incident
13. Update the service pipeline

Your worker now needs to do:

Event
 ↓
Correlator
 ↓
Cluster
 ↓
Attack Intelligence
 ↓
Risk Score
 ↓
Update Incident
 ↓
Publish live analysis

Modify the service:

from security_response.intelligence import (
    AttackIntelligence,
)


class SecurityResponseService:

    def __init__(
        self,
        session,
        publisher,
    ):

        self.repository = (
            SecurityResponseRepository(
                session
            )
        )

        self.publisher = publisher

        self.intelligence = (
            AttackIntelligence()
        )

    async def process_cluster(
        self,
        incident_id: str,
        events,
    ):

        await self.publisher.publish(
            event_type="attack_analysis_started",
            incident_id=incident_id,
            data={
                "event_count": len(events),
            },
        )

        assessment = (
            self.intelligence.analyze(
                events
            )
        )

        incident = (
            await self.repository
            .update_assessment(
                incident_id,
                assessment,
            )
        )

        await self.publisher.publish(
            event_type="attack_analysis_complete",
            incident_id=incident_id,
            data={
                "attack_type":
                    assessment.attack_type.value,
                "severity":
                    assessment.severity,
                "confidence":
                    assessment.confidence,
                "risk_score":
                    assessment.risk_score,
                "attack_stage":
                    assessment.attack_stage,
                "mitre_techniques":
                    assessment.mitre_techniques,
                "evidence":
                    assessment.evidence.observations,
            },
        )

        return assessment

Then later:

15. A critical addition: confidence vs risk

Don't combine these into one value.

Example:

Case A

Confidence: 98%
Risk: 35

Known attack, but low impact.

versus:

Case B

Confidence: 63%
Risk: 91

Uncertain classification,
but potentially catastrophic impact.

Aariya should treat those differently.

The next response engine can then use both:

confidence
+
impact
+
affected assets
+
attack stage
+
persistence
+
response risk

rather than simply:

if score > 70:
    block()
