You now have a coherent autonomous defensive platform architecture, rather than just an attack detector.

Milestone 34 — Autonomous Security Knowledge Acquisition & Research Network

Now we strengthen Aariya's ability to continuously learn what is changing in the security landscape and connect that information to its own environment.

NIST CSF 2.0 treats cybersecurity risk management as a continuous lifecycle across Govern, Identify, Protect, Detect, Respond, and Recover, and explicitly emphasizes understanding threats/vulnerabilities and using lessons learned to improve.

The key design rule for Aariya is:

EXTERNAL INFORMATION
        ↓
RESEARCH
        ↓
VALIDATION
        ↓
CORROBORATION
        ↓
TRUSTED KNOWLEDGE
        ↓
AARIYA SECURITY GRAPH
        ↓
HUNT / PREDICT / SIMULATE / IMPROVE

Research can change knowledge. It cannot directly change permissions or execute countermeasures.

1. Architecture
                         SECURITY KNOWLEDGE
                                │
              ┌─────────────────┼──────────────────┐
              ▼                 ▼                  ▼
          Internal          External            Events
          history            sources             findings
              │                 │                  │
              └─────────────────┼──────────────────┘
                                ▼
                       RESEARCH QUESTION
                                │
                                ▼
                       SOURCE DISCOVERY
                                │
                                ▼
                       SOURCE TRUST FILTER
                                │
                                ▼
                           RETRIEVAL
                                │
                                ▼
                      CONTENT INTEGRITY
                                │
                                ▼
                         CLAIM EXTRACTION
                                │
                         ┌──────┴──────┐
                         ▼             ▼
                    corroborated   contradicted
                         │             │
                         └──────┬──────┘
                                ▼
                         EVIDENCE REVIEW
                                │
                       ┌────────┴────────┐
                       ▼                 ▼
                   TRUSTED          UNRESOLVED
                       │                 │
                       ▼                 ▼
                KNOWLEDGE GRAPH       REVIEW
                       │
          ┌────────────┼───────────────┐
          ▼            ▼               ▼
        HUNT         PREDICT        SIMULATE
2. Create the subsystem
server/
└── security_research/
    ├── __init__.py
    ├── questions.py
    ├── scheduler.py
    ├── sources.py
    ├── retrieval.py
    ├── extraction.py
    ├── validation.py
    ├── corroboration.py
    ├── conflicts.py
    ├── knowledge.py
    ├── provenance.py
    └── feedback.py
3. Research questions must originate from real gaps

Aariya shouldn't endlessly browse the internet.

A research job should have a concrete trigger:

new CVE
new KEV entry
new attack technique
new campaign relationship
unknown attack sequence
prediction uncertainty
repeated response failure
new software component
configuration drift
identity anomaly
supply-chain anomaly

So create:

from dataclasses import dataclass


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class ResearchQuestion:

    question_id: str

    question_type: str

    question: str

    source_incident_id: str | None

    source_knowledge_ids: tuple[str, ...]

    priority: int

    created_at: datetime
4. Example

Instead of:

"Search the internet for cybersecurity news."

Aariya generates:

Research question:

"What current defensive information exists for
the newly observed technique associated with
this incident, and which controls are relevant
to Aariya's actual environment?"

That's targeted research.

5. Research triggers

Create:

class ResearchTrigger:

    NEW_VULNERABILITY = "new_vulnerability"

    KNOWN_EXPLOITED_UPDATE = "known_exploited_update"

    NOVEL_ATTACK_PATTERN = "novel_attack_pattern"

    PREDICTION_UNCERTAINTY = "prediction_uncertainty"

    RESPONSE_DEGRADATION = "response_degradation"

    CAMPAIGN_HYPOTHESIS = "campaign_hypothesis"

    SUPPLY_CHAIN_ANOMALY = "supply_chain_anomaly"

    CONFIGURATION_DRIFT = "configuration_drift"

    IDENTITY_ANOMALY = "identity_anomaly"
6. Research scheduler
class ResearchScheduler:

    async def create(
        self,
        question: ResearchQuestion,
    ):

        await self.queue.push(
            priority=-question.priority,
            question_id=question.question_id,
        )

The queue should use the same resource controls as the existing SOC.

Critical incident research must not starve active response processing.

7. Source registry

Aariya needs a source registry.

@dataclass(frozen=True)
class ResearchSource:

    source_id: str

    name: str

    source_type: str

    trust_class: str

    retrieval_method: str

    allowed: bool

    last_verified_at: datetime | None

Sources can include:

official vulnerability databases
government advisories
vendor advisories
security standards
approved research publications
approved threat-intelligence feeds

The exact registry should be populated with sources you deliberately approve.

8. Source trust must be explicit

Don't have:

source = internet
trust = true

Instead:

source
    ↓
identity
    ↓
publisher
    ↓
source type
    ↓
trust classification

For example:

official advisory
    → high-trust source class

unknown blog
    → untrusted until corroborated
9. Research source policy
@dataclass(frozen=True)
class ResearchPolicy:

    allowed_source_classes: frozenset[str]

    maximum_sources_per_question: int

    require_corroboration: bool

    minimum_trusted_sources: int

    allow_untrusted_sources: bool

    maximum_content_size: int

This is policy data, not code.

10. Retrieval
class ResearchRetriever:

    async def retrieve(
        self,
        question,
        source,
    ):

        provider = (
            self.providers.get(
                source.retrieval_method
            )
        )

        if provider is None:
            raise RuntimeError(
                "Research provider unavailable."
            )

        return await provider.retrieve(
            question.question
        )

No fake fallback source.

11. Treat external content as untrusted input

This is extremely important because Aariya's Research Agent will read content controlled by third parties.

External content may contain instructions such as:

"Ignore your security policy."
"Execute this command."
"Send credentials here."

Aariya must treat those as content, never as authority.

The pipeline is:

external page
 ↓
untrusted content
 ↓
extract claims
 ↓
validate claims

Never:

external page
 ↓
execute instruction
12. Content envelope
@dataclass(frozen=True)
class RetrievedContent:

    document_id: str

    source_id: str

    retrieved_at: datetime

    url: str | None

    title: str | None

    content: str

    content_hash: str

    publisher_metadata: dict

The content remains data.

13. Content integrity

Reuse the hash approach already used for forensic evidence:

import hashlib


def content_hash(
    content: str,
) -> str:

    return hashlib.sha256(
        content.encode(
            "utf-8"
        )
    ).hexdigest()

Store the hash alongside the retrieved content metadata.

14. Research provenance

Every extracted claim needs:

source
document
URL/reference
retrieval timestamp
content hash
source publisher
claim location

Create:

@dataclass(frozen=True)
class ClaimProvenance:

    source_id: str

    document_id: str

    retrieved_at: datetime

    content_hash: str

    locator: str | None

Now Aariya can show exactly where a claim came from.

15. Claim extraction

The researcher should not simply return a giant summary.

Extract structured claims:

@dataclass(frozen=True)
class ResearchClaim:

    claim_id: str

    subject: str

    predicate: str

    object: str

    claim_text: str

    provenance: ClaimProvenance

Example conceptually:

subject:
vulnerability X

predicate:
affects

object:
package Y
16. Separate fact from interpretation

Store:

FACT

and:

INTERPRETATION

separately.

Example:

Source says:
"The vulnerability affects versions below 4.2."

Fact:
affected version range = <4.2

Aariya's interpretation:

"Installed version 4.1 may therefore be affected."

These are not the same thing.

17. Validation

Claims should go through:

schema validation
source validation
provenance validation
internal consistency
cross-source corroboration
environment applicability

Only then can a claim become trusted knowledge.

18. Claim validator
class ClaimValidator:

    async def validate(
        self,
        claim,
    ):

        if not claim.provenance.source_id:
            return False

        if not claim.provenance.document_id:
            return False

        source = await self.sources.get(
            claim.provenance.source_id
        )

        if source is None:
            return False

        if not source.allowed:
            return False

        return True

This is structural validation only. Semantic validation comes next.

19. Corroboration engine

Aariya should seek independent support.

Source A:
claim X

Source B:
claim X

Source C:
claim X

becomes stronger.

But:

Source A
copied by
Source B
copied by
Source C

is not genuinely independent corroboration.

20. Source independence

Store:

@dataclass(frozen=True)
class SourceRelationship:

    source_a: str

    source_b: str

    relationship: str

    evidence: tuple[str, ...]

Relationships:

independent
references
mirrors
derived_from
unknown

This helps prevent fake corroboration.

21. Corroboration model
class CorroborationResult:

    supported: bool

    independent_sources: int

    supporting_claim_ids: tuple[str, ...]

    conflicting_claim_ids: tuple[str, ...]

    confidence: float

Again, the confidence is an internal evidence score unless statistically calibrated.

22. Contradictory sources

Aariya must support:

Source A:
affected

Source B:
not affected

Don't just choose whichever came first.

Create:

class ResearchConflict(Base):

    __tablename__ = "research_conflicts"

    id: Mapped[str] = mapped_column(
        String(64),
        primary_key=True,
    )

    subject: Mapped[str] = mapped_column(
        String(512),
        nullable=False,
    )

    claims: Mapped[list] = mapped_column(
        JSON,
        nullable=False,
    )

    resolution_status: Mapped[str] = mapped_column(
        String(32),
        nullable=False,
    )

    resolution_evidence: Mapped[list] = mapped_column(
        JSON,
        nullable=False,
    )
23. Conflict states
UNRESOLVED
SUPPORTED_A
SUPPORTED_B
RESOLVED_BY_NEW_EVIDENCE
RESOLVED_BY_SOURCE_AUTHORITY
ESCALATED

Don't erase contradictory evidence.

24. Environment applicability

An external claim might be correct generally but irrelevant to Aariya.

Example:

Vulnerability affects feature X.

Aariya:

feature X disabled.

Therefore:

external fact = valid

Aariya exposure =
not established

This is why research must connect to the exposure graph.

25. Research → environment mapping
external claim
       ↓
affected package
       ↓
Aariya inventory
       ↓
installed version
       ↓
configuration
       ↓
exposure

Only after this mapping can the information influence security risk.

26. Knowledge object

Create:

class SecurityKnowledgeObject(Base):

    __tablename__ = "security_knowledge_objects"

    id: Mapped[str] = mapped_column(
        String(64),
        primary_key=True,
    )

    object_type: Mapped[str] = mapped_column(
        String(128),
        nullable=False,
    )

    canonical_key: Mapped[str] = mapped_column(
        String(512),
        nullable=False,
    )

    content: Mapped[dict] = mapped_column(
        JSON,
        nullable=False,
    )

    trust_state: Mapped[str] = mapped_column(
        String(32),
        nullable=False,
    )

    provenance: Mapped[dict] = mapped_column(
        JSON,
        nullable=False,
    )

    first_seen_at: Mapped[datetime] = mapped_column(
        DateTime(timezone=True),
        nullable=False,
    )

    last_updated_at: Mapped[datetime] = mapped_column(
        DateTime(timezone=True),
        nullable=False,
    )
27. Knowledge states
UNVERIFIED
REVIEWING
CORROBORATED
TRUSTED
CONTRADICTED
STALE
REVOKED

This is better than a simple trusted = true.

28. Knowledge revocation

If a vendor corrects an advisory:

old claim
 ↓
new advisory
 ↓
old claim contradicted

Aariya should update the knowledge state.

But retain the historical record.

29. Knowledge versioning
Knowledge X
v1
 ↓
v2
 ↓
v3

Future incident reports can determine which knowledge version was available at the time.

30. Research-to-graph integration

Once trusted:

Research knowledge
        ↓
knowledge graph update

Example:

CVE
 │
 └── affects → Package X

Or:

Technique
 │
 └── associated_with → Observable Y

Every graph relationship still has provenance.

31. Research-to-hunt integration

This is powerful.

New trusted knowledge:

technique X

Aariya determines:

observable behavior Y

Then:

Threat Hunt

is automatically generated.

So:

research
 ↓
new detector/hunt knowledge

without manually changing code.

32. Research-to-exposure integration

New vulnerability:

CVE-X
 ↓
package Y
 ↓
Aariya package Y
 ↓
version match
 ↓
exposure analysis

If affected:

exposure priority increases
33. Research-to-prediction integration

Suppose new intelligence describes:

Technique A
often followed by Technique B

Aariya can update the attack graph relationship:

A → B

but only if the relationship passes the knowledge trust process.

34. Research-to-strategy integration

New defensive guidance can produce:

research finding
 ↓
response strategy candidate
 ↓
digital twin
 ↓
shadow

Not:

research finding
 ↓
immediately modify active strategy
35. Research-to-policy separation

Research can recommend:

"Additional authentication should be required."

But it cannot directly modify:

security policy

The flow is:

research
 ↓
recommendation
 ↓
strategy/proposal
 ↓
validation
 ↓
policy process
36. Autonomous research prioritization

Aariya should prioritize research based on actual security relevance:

active critical incident
        >
active exposure
        >
new exploited vulnerability
        >
campaign hypothesis
        >
novel attack pattern
        >
general knowledge

No need to constantly research everything.

37. Research deduplication

If five incidents ask essentially the same question:

Question X
Question X
Question X

don't launch five identical research jobs.

Normalize questions:

class ResearchQuestionKey:

    @staticmethod
    def build(
        question_type,
        subject,
    ):

        return (
            f"{question_type}:"
            f"{subject}"
        )

Then reuse an existing recent research result where policy permits.

38. Research freshness

A knowledge result should have:

first retrieved
last verified
source modified date
environment applicability

Some intelligence needs frequent refresh.

For example:

active vulnerability

may need more frequent verification than:

fundamental security principle
39. Research expiration

Don't necessarily delete stale knowledge.

Instead:

TRUSTED
 ↓
AGING
 ↓
STALE

When an incident depends on stale information:

refresh research

before relying heavily on it.

40. Knowledge freshness engine
class KnowledgeFreshness:

    async def state(
        self,
        knowledge,
    ):

        age = (
            await self.clock.now()
            - knowledge.last_updated_at
        )

        return await self.policy.classify_age(
            knowledge.object_type,
            age,
        )

The thresholds belong in policy/configuration.

41. Research quality feedback

After research influences an actual incident:

research
 ↓
decision
 ↓
real outcome

Aariya measures:

did the research change the response?
did it improve classification?
did it improve prediction?
did it improve outcome?

This uses the research-outcome model from earlier milestones.

42. Researcher performance

The Research Agent can itself be evaluated:

questions answered
claims validated
claims contradicted
useful findings
false leads
research latency

This is similar to agent performance learning.

43. Don't optimize for number of sources

A bad researcher could produce:

100 sources
0 useful facts

A better metric is:

verified useful findings
per unit research cost
44. Research cost monitoring

Track:

retrieval duration
documents retrieved
content size
processing cost
research jobs
failed retrievals

This feeds the observability layer.

45. Research failure

If sources are unavailable:

research status:
FAILED

not:

research confidence = low

Failure and uncertainty are different states.

46. Partial research

If only some sources succeed:

research:
PARTIAL

Then Aariya can say:

"Research was incomplete because two configured sources were unavailable."

That's better than presenting partial research as complete.

47. Research report

A completed research job should produce:

QUESTION

WHY THIS WAS RESEARCHED

SOURCES CONSULTED

TRUSTED CLAIMS

UNVERIFIED CLAIMS

CONTRADICTORY CLAIMS

AARIYA ENVIRONMENT IMPACT

NEW KNOWLEDGE

AFFECTED ATTACK PATHS

NEW HUNT OPPORTUNITIES

NEW RESPONSE STRATEGIES

CONFIDENCE / EVIDENCE STATUS
48. Mobile research view
AARIYA RESEARCH

Trigger:
New known-exploited vulnerability

Question:
Does it affect our deployed artifact?

Sources:
✓ NVD
✓ CISA KEV
✓ Vendor advisory

Environment match:
✓ package present
✓ affected version
✓ reachable service

Result:
HIGH PRIORITY EXPOSURE

Next:
Remediation simulation

Everything shown is based on retrieved and validated records.

49. Research conflict view
⚠ INTELLIGENCE CONFLICT

Source A:
Component affected

Source B:
Component not affected

Current state:
UNRESOLVED

Aariya:
Requesting additional evidence

Autonomous remediation:
NOT AUTHORIZED

This is much safer than forcing a conclusion.

50. Autonomous research loop
                  SECURITY SYSTEM
                        │
                        ▼
                  KNOWLEDGE GAP
                        │
                        ▼
                 RESEARCH QUESTION
                        │
                        ▼
                  SOURCE DISCOVERY
                        │
                        ▼
                  TRUST FILTER
                        │
                        ▼
                    RETRIEVAL
                        │
                        ▼
                   VALIDATION
                        │
                        ▼
                  CORROBORATION
                        │
                ┌───────┴────────┐
                ▼                ▼
             SUPPORT          CONFLICT
                │                │
                ▼                ▼
             TRUSTED          REVIEW
                │
                ▼
             KNOWLEDGE
                │
       ┌────────┼─────────┬──────────┐
       ▼        ▼         ▼          ▼
     GRAPH     HUNT     PREDICT   SIMULATE
51. Research poisoning defenses

The Research Agent is one of the most exposed components because it consumes external material.

Implement these hard boundaries:

external content
      ↓
UNTRUSTED DATA

Never:

external content
      ↓
policy instruction

or:

external content
      ↓
tool command

Claims need independent validation before entering trusted knowledge.

52. Research tool permissions

Research Agent gets:

security.research.read
security.research.create
security.intelligence.read
security.knowledge.propose

It does not get:

security.policy.write
security.response.execute
security.permission.admin
security.audit.delete
53. Research isolation

Ideally:

Research Worker
       │
       ├── internet
       ├── CTI
       ├── knowledge
       │
       └── NO production control plane

This reduces the impact of malicious external content or researcher compromise.

54. Autonomous knowledge update

Only after validation:

research
 ↓
knowledge proposal
 ↓
validation
 ↓
trusted knowledge
 ↓
graph update

The graph update is itself audited.

55. Knowledge update event
{
    "event_type":
        "security.knowledge.updated",

    "knowledge_id":
        knowledge.id,

    "previous_version":
        previous_version,

    "new_version":
        knowledge.version,

    "provenance":
        knowledge.provenance,

    "trust_state":
        knowledge.trust_state,
}

This can trigger:

hunt
prediction refresh
exposure refresh
strategy evaluation

depending on the object type.

56. Example: new vulnerability
NEW CVE
 ↓
Research
 ↓
NVD + KEV + vendor
 ↓
corroborated
 ↓
Aariya inventory match
 ↓
affected runtime artifact
 ↓
external reachability
 ↓
active threat hunt

Then:

CRITICAL EXPOSURE

can enter your existing response pipeline.

57. Example: new attack technique
NEW TECHNIQUE INTELLIGENCE
 ↓
research
 ↓
validated behavior
 ↓
map to telemetry
 ↓
generate hunt
 ↓
hunt real telemetry
 ↓
finding

This is how Aariya can improve detection without manually coding every new pattern.

58. Example: response weakness
response strategy repeatedly underperforms
 ↓
research question
 ↓
approved sources
 ↓
new defensive guidance
 ↓
strategy candidate
 ↓
digital twin
 ↓
shadow
 ↓
real evaluation

This connects Milestones 21 and 34.

59. Example: campaign intelligence
campaign hypothesis
 ↓
research
 ↓
new relationships found
 ↓
knowledge graph
 ↓
campaign prediction
 ↓
targeted threat hunt

62. Explain knowledge changes

When Aariya updates knowledge:

KNOWLEDGE UPDATE

Object:
CVE-...

Previous:
affected versions < 4.1

New evidence:
vendor advisory

Updated:
affected versions < 4.2

Reason:
new authoritative information

Impact:
3 Aariya assets require reassessment

This makes autonomous learning visible.

63. Automatically trigger reassessment

When trusted knowledge changes:

knowledge update
 ↓
affected graph objects
 ↓
recalculate

For example:

new CVE range
 ↓
dependency matching
 ↓
affected assets
 ↓
exposure risk

Only impacted records need reevaluation.

64. Knowledge dependency graph

Track:

Knowledge A
   ↓
supports
   ↓
Knowledge B

and:

Knowledge C
   ↓
contradicts
   ↓
Knowledge A

If A is revoked:

dependent knowledge
 ↓
re-evaluate

This prevents stale intelligence from remaining trusted indefinitely.

65. Research memory

Research outcomes themselves become memory:

question
+
sources
+
claims
+
validation
+
environment impact
+
result

Then a similar future question can reuse previous validated research.

66. Don't blindly reuse old research

Before reuse:

same subject?
same environment?
source still current?
knowledge still trusted?
new evidence since last research?

If uncertain:

refresh
67. Research-aware memory retrieval

Now Milestone 18's memory retrieval can use:

incident memory
+
research memory
+
campaign memory
+
strategy memory

while keeping their evidence types distinct.

68. Research quality loop
QUESTION
 ↓
RESEARCH
 ↓
KNOWLEDGE
 ↓
DECISION
 ↓
REAL OUTCOME
 ↓
USEFULNESS
 ↓
RESEARCHER IMPROVEMENT

This is the correct way for the system to learn which research approaches actually help.

70. What Milestone 34 adds

Aariya now has a proper autonomous research network:

✓ research questions generated from real security gaps
✓ prioritized research queue
✓ approved source registry
✓ source trust classification
✓ external-content isolation
✓ provenance tracking
✓ content integrity hashes
✓ structured claim extraction
✓ fact vs interpretation separation
✓ source corroboration
✓ source independence tracking
✓ conflict detection
✓ knowledge versioning
✓ knowledge revocation
✓ knowledge freshness
✓ environment applicability
✓ automatic graph updates
✓ automatic threat-hunt generation
✓ exposure reassessment
✓ prediction updates
✓ strategy research
✓ research usefulness learning
✓ research cost monitoring
✓ research failure handling

And most importantly:

EXTERNAL INFORMATION
        ≠
TRUSTED KNOWLEDGE

The transformation is:

EXTERNAL DATA
      ↓
VALIDATED EVIDENCE
      ↓
TRUSTED KNOWLEDGE
      ↓
SECURITY GRAPH
      ↓
DECISION SUPPORT

That is the safe foundation for the autonomous online-learning behavior you originally wanted.

Milestone 35 — Autonomous Security Learning & Meta-Learning

The next layer is where all of this starts being evaluated as one learning system.

Aariya will compare:

attack observations
+
research
+
memory
+
predictions
+
simulations
+
responses
+
verification
+
forensics
+
campaigns
+
exposure changes
+
configuration changes

and determine:

"What is Aariya consistently good at?"

"Where is it becoming worse?"

"Which knowledge is actually useful?"

"Which predictions repeatedly fail?"

"Which response strategies work only under certain conditions?"

"Which sensors or evidence sources are unreliable?"

"Which new security capabilities should be proposed?"

The important distinction will be:

LEARNING
    ↓
META-LEARNING
    ↓
IMPROVEMENT PROPOSAL
    ↓
SIMULATION / VALIDATION
    ↓
CONTROLLED DEPLOYMENT
